Shortly after exit polls were released on the night of 7 July, Macron's Prime Minister, Gabriel Attal, announced he would tender his resignation to the President. The following day, Macron refused Attal's resignation, instead asking him to stay on until a new government could be formed.[^cite_note-317]

On 27 August, Macron refused to appoint NFP-designate [Lucie Castets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucie_Castets) as prime minister.[^cite_note-318]

After holding talks with leaders from parties represented in Parliament in an effort to find a working majority, Macron appointed conservative figure [Michel Barnier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michel_Barnier) as Prime Minister on 5 September and invited him to form a "unity government". Barnier's appointment was seen as a growing sign of Le Pen's influence on French politics since she had previously blocked the appointment of [Xavier Bertrand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xavier_Bertrand), another conservative figure seen as a serious contender for the premiership.[^cite_note-319]

##### Vote of no-confidence in the Barnier government

In early December 2024, Prime Minister Michel Barnier resorted to Article 49.3 of the Constitution to pass the 2025 Social Security budget without parliamentary approval. In response to Barnier's move, the National Assembly approved a vote of no confidence on 4 December, effectively bringing down the government. Barnier resigned, but Macron vowed to stay in office until the end of his term.[^cite_note-320] It was the first French cabinet to be toppled by Parliament since 1962.[^cite_note-321] Macron addressed the country on 5 December. He accepted the resignation of Barnier but vowed to remain in office until the end of his term. He blamed an alliance of 'extreme left and extreme right' for the collapse of the French government.[^cite_note-322]

#### Foreign affairs



Macron with Italian Prime Minister [Giorgia Meloni](29228976.md) in October 2022



Macron, President of the European Commission [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md) and Chinese President [Xi Jinping](2017814.md) at the [2023 France–China Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_France%E2%80%93China_Summit)

On 16 June 2022, Macron visited Ukraine alongside German Chancellor [Olaf Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz) and Italy's Prime Minister [Mario Draghi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_Draghi). He met with Ukraine's President [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) and expressed "European Unity" for Ukraine.[^cite_note-323][^cite_note-324] He said that the nations that remained [neutral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement) in the [Russo-Ukrainian War](42085878.md) made a historic mistake and were complicit in the [new imperialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Imperialism).[^cite_note-325]



Macron with Brazilian President [Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva](99328.md) in May 2023

In September 2022, Macron criticized the United States, [Norway](21241.md) and other "friendly" [natural gas supplier states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_natural_gas_exports) for the extremely high prices of their supplies,[^cite_note-326] saying in October 2022 that Europeans are "paying four times as much as the price at which you sell to your industry. That is not exactly the meaning of friendship."[^cite_note-327]

Macron and his wife attended the [state funeral of Queen Elizabeth II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_and_state_funeral_of_Elizabeth_II) in Westminster Abbey, London, on 19 September 2022, and the [coronation of King Charles III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronation_of_Charles_III_and_Camilla) the following year.[^cite_note-328][^cite_note-329]

On 23 October 2022, Macron became the first foreign leader to meet new Italian Prime Minister [Giorgia Meloni](29228976.md), just a day after she and her [ministers](72067724.md) were sworn into office.[^cite_note-330]

During a [summit to China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_France-China_Summit) with European Commission President [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md), which included a formal meeting with [Xi Jinping](2017814.md), the [General Secretary of the Chinese Communist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Secretary_of_the_Chinese_Communist_Party) and [President of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_China), Macron called for Europe to reduce its dependence on the [United States](3434750.md) in general and to stay neutral and avoid being drawn into any possible confrontation between the U.S. and [China](5405.md) over [Taiwan](25734.md). Speaking after a three-day state visit to China, Macron emphasised his theory of [strategic autonomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strategic_autonomy), suggesting that Europe could become a "[third superpower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potential_superpower#European_Union)". He argued that Europe should focus on boosting its own defence industries and additionally reduce its dependence on the [United States dollar](18717338.md) (USD).[^cite_note-peuja-331] in a follow-up speech in [The Hague](30269.md) to further outline his vision of strategic autonomy for Europe.[^cite_note-f24out-332] On 7 June 2023, a report by the [pan-European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-European_identity) [think tank](37101.md) [European Council on Foreign Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Council_on_Foreign_Relations) (ECFR) found that most Europeans agree with Macron's views on China and the United States.[^cite_note-333]

In February 2023, he welcomed Ethiopian Prime Minister [Abiy Ahmed](56624029.md) in Paris to normalize relations between France and [Ethiopia](187749.md), strained by the [Tigray War](65760352.md) between the Ethiopian government and [Tigray](357350.md) rebels.[^cite_note-334]



Macron at the [NATO Summit in Vilnius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Vilnius_NATO_summit) on 12 July 2023

On 31 May 2023 Macron visited the GLOBSEC forum in Bratislava, where he again delivered a speech on European sovereignty.[^cite_note-f24slv-335] During the question and answer session that followed the Bratislava speech,[^cite_note-336] he said that negotiating with Putin may have to take priority over any war crimes tribunal which some others, including Zelensky, wish to see.[^cite_note-pwput-337]

On 12 June 2023, Macron promised to deliver more ammunition, weapons and armed vehicles to help Ukrainian forces with the ongoing [counter-offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Ukrainian_counteroffensive) to liberate Russian-[occupied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts) southeastern [Ukraine](31750.md).[^cite_note-338] At the [NATO Summit in Vilnius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Vilnius_summit), he promised to supply Ukraine with [Scalp long-range cruise missiles](306908.md) to hit Russian targets deep behind the front lines.[^cite_note-339] On 10 November 2023, he said that what Russia is doing in Ukraine is "imperialism and colonialism" and it was the "duty" of France and other countries to help Ukraine defend itself, but added that maybe the time will come to hold fair [peace negotiations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_negotiations_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) and find a solution with Russia.[^cite_note-340]

In June 2023 Macron hosted a global climate finance conference described by many as the new [Bretton Woods Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bretton_Woods_Conference). The purpose is to adjust the global economy to the contemporary threats of [climate change](5042951.md) and hunger. One of the propositions is to offer low income countries aid instead of credit so they can use their resources for stopping climate change and poverty instead of debt payments. Macron supported the idea, but a climate activist from Uganda remarked that the promises were meaningless if at the same time Macron supported projects such as the [East African Crude Oil Pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_African_Crude_Oil_Pipeline), a major threat to the climate and to the drinking water of 40 million people.[^cite_note-341] At the summit Macron proposed an [international taxation system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_taxation) and [debt restructuring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Debt_restructuring) but stressed it can have an effect only with [international cooperation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_cooperation).[^cite_note-342]



Macron with Israeli President [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md) in Jerusalem, Israel, 24 October 2023

In July 2023, Macron postponed his planned state visit to Germany due to the ongoing [Nahel M. riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahel_M._riots).[^cite_note-343]

In October 2023, Macron condemned [Hamas](13913.md)'s actions during the [Gaza war](74998519.md) and expressed his support to [Israel](9282173.md) and its right to self-defense.[^cite_note-344][^cite_note-345] On 10 November 2023, he called for a ceasefire and urged Israel to stop [bombing Gaza and killing civilians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_crimes_in_the_2023_Israel%E2%80%93Hamas_war).[^cite_note-346] In July 2025, Macron announced that at the next meeting of the [UN General Assembly](31957.md), France would officially recognise a Palestinian state.[^cite_note-347]

In February 2024, during a meeting with other European states, Macron generated controversy by suggesting [sending ground troops to Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine%E2%80%93NATO_relations).[^cite_note-348] On 28 May 2024, Macron gave Ukraine permission to use [SCALP EG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SCALP_EG) missiles against targets on Russian soil. Such usage was instructed to be limited "to neutralize military sites from which missiles are being fired, military sites from which Ukraine is being attacked".[^cite_note-349] On 24 February 2025, during a meeting with US President [Donald Trump](4848272.md), Macron said that a [truce between Ukraine and Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_negotiations_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) could be agreed in the coming weeks.[^cite_note-350] On 22 February 2025, Macron pledged to continue its supply of defense equipment to [Armenia](10918072.md) after the 2020 Nagorno-Karabakh war, as well as humanitarian aid of 29 million euros to [displaced Armenians from Nagorno-Karabakh](74922032.md). He also stated support for the [International Court of Justice](14918.md)'s decision on 17 November 2023, of which calls on the rights of ethnic Armenian residents to return safely to their homeland.[^cite_note-351]



German Chancellor [Friedrich Merz](1967626.md), Macron, British Prime Minister [Keir Starmer](18681491.md) and Polish Prime Minister [Donald Tusk](648724.md) with Ukrainian President [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) in [Ukraine](31750.md) in May 2025

In February 2025, Macron characterised Russia as an "existential threat" to Europe, convening a special meeting in Paris to coordinate European responses under the [Weimar+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weimar+) framework.[^cite_note-352] In March 2025, German lawmakers approved an [amendment to the Basic Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_balanced_budget_amendment) that would allow [Friedrich Merz](1967626.md)'s government to implement the most massive rearmament of Germany since [World War II](32927.md).[^cite_note-353] Germany's decision to massively [increase defense spending](79277430.md) was welcomed by Macron.[^cite_note-354]

Following the [Israeli strikes on Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_2025_Israeli_strikes_on_Iran) on 13 June 2025, Macron called for "maximum restraint" while reaffirming "Israel's right to defend itself and ensure its security."[^cite_note-355] On 17 June 2025, he claimed that president Trump's haste departure from the [G7 summit](1196634.md), was to reach a cease fire between Israel and Iran. To which Trump responded "Wrong! He has no idea why I am now on my way to Washington, but it certainly has nothing to do with a Cease Fire. Much bigger than that. Whether purposely or not, Emmanuel always gets it wrong. Stay Tuned!".[^cite_note-356] On 23 June 2025, Macron called Trump's [air strikes on Iranian nuclear sites](80262844.md) "illegal" and counterproductive,[^cite_note-357] and called for the resumption of diplomatic talks with Iran.[^cite_note-358]

In 2026, speaking at the Africa Forward Summit in Nairobi, Kenya, Macron called for an immediate ceasefire in the [Sudanese civil war](73556699.md) and an end to external support for the belligerents. He stated that the conflict was being fuelled by external forces financing the warring parties and described the situation as involving [war crimes](74944426.md) and a severe [humanitarian crisis](73745525.md).[^cite_note-359] During his presidency, France faced criticism after [Amnesty International](18947898.md) reported that French-manufactured military systems were being used by Sudan’s [Rapid Support Forces](43053874.md) (RSF) on armoured vehicles supplied by the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md).[^cite_note-360] Amnesty called on France to suspend further transfers of the equipment to the United Arab Emirates.[^cite_note-361]



Macron and U.S. President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) at the UN General Assembly in New York City, 23 September 2025

In January 2026, following the [capture of Nicolás Maduro by U.S. forces](82021123.md), Macron formally called for [Edmundo González](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmundo_Gonz%C3%A1lez) to lead a "peaceful, democratic" transition in [Venezuela](32374.md).[^cite_note-362] Macron praised the removal of the "Maduro dictatorship," stating that the Venezuelan people "can only rejoice" at being rid of his rule.[^cite_note-363]

On 9 January 2026, Macron, along with British Prime Minister [Keir Starmer](18681491.md) and German Chancellor [Friedrich Merz](1967626.md), issued a joint statement addressing the escalating [anti-government protests in Iran](81976180.md), which had started in late December 2025. In their statement, they called on the Iranian authorities to exercise restraint and to avoid further [violence](82036668.md).[^cite_note-364]

During his speech at the [World Economic Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/56th_World_Economic_Forum) in Davos on 20 January 2026, Macron emphasized the importance of increasing Chinese [foreign direct investment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment) (FDI) in key European sectors to promote economic growth and facilitate technology transfer. He urged [China](5405.md) to move beyond simply exporting products to Europe and instead focus on local manufacturing and physical presence on the continent.[^cite_note-365]



Macron, Syrian President [Ahmed al-Sharaa](44007699.md), and Syrian Foreign Minister [Asaad al-Shaibani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asaad_al-Shaibani), 24 April 2026

In March 2026, Macron described the [U.S.–Israeli strikes on Iran](82533582.md) as illegal, while maintaining that Iran held "primary responsibility" for the regional escalation. Despite criticizing the strikes, he reaffirmed France's commitment to regional allies and deployed the aircraft carrier *[Charles de Gaulle](639127.md)* to the Mediterranean for defensive and escort missions.[^cite_note-366] In September 2026, Macron announced that the country will send soldiers to [protect](52395034.md) Saudi Arabia on a [Red Sea](26191.md) oil route, as part of an accord with Saudis.[^cite_note-367]

#### Controversies

##### Uber Files

Main article: [Uber Files](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uber_Files)

On 10 July 2022, *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* revealed that Macron had assisted Uber in lobbying during his term as the Minister of Economics and Finance,[^cite_note-GuardianUberFiles-368] leading to calls from opposition lawmakers for a parliamentary inquiry.[^cite_note-UberFilesInquiryCalls-369][^cite_note-UberDefence-370] In his own defence, Macron expressed that he "did his job" and that he would "do it again tomorrow and the day after tomorrow".[^cite_note-UberDefence-370] He stated, "I'm proud of it".[^cite_note-UberDefence-370]

### Co-prince of Andorra

As president of France, Macron also serves *ex officio* as one of the two [co-princes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Co-Prince_of_Andorra) of [Andorra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andorra). His chief of staff [Patrick Strzoda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Strzoda) serves as his representative in this capacity. [Joan Enric Vives i Sicília](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joan_Enric_Vives_i_Sic%C3%ADlia), appointed as the [Bishop of Urgell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bishop_of_Urgell) on 12 May 2003, served as Macron's co-prince until [Josep-Lluís Serrano Pentinat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josep-Llu%C3%ADs_Serrano_Pentinat) succeeded him in 2025.[^cite_note-371] Macron swore the [Constitution of Andorra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Andorra) through Strzoda in an act that took place on 15 June 2017 in [Casa de la Vall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casa_de_la_Vall).[^cite_note-372] Macron visited the country on a State visit on 12 September 2019. The following day he spoke at the historic building of the [General Council of Andorra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Council_\(Andorra\)) alongside Episcopal Co-Prince Joan Enric Vives, Prime Minister [Xavier Espot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xavier_Espot) and the General Syndic [Roser Suñé Pascuet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roser_Su%C3%B1%C3%A9_Pascuet).[^cite_note-373]

During the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md), the Andorran government asked France for [economic aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aid), but Macron refused, arguing that the [Bank of France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_France) could not offer loans to another country without the approval of the [European Central Bank](9474.md).[^cite_note-374]

In January 2024, former New Caledonian high commissioner [Patrice Faure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrice_Faure) was appointed chief of staff to Macron,[^cite_note-375] and in November, his representative to [Andorra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andorra).[^cite_note-376]

## Political positions

Main article: [Political positions of Emmanuel Macron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_positions_of_Emmanuel_Macron)

Macron's political views have been described as [moderate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moderate), [centrist](39619035.md), and [pragmatic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pragmatic_politics).

## Personal life



Emmanuel Macron and his wife [Brigitte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brigitte_Macron) in 2017

Macron is married to [Brigitte Trogneux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brigitte_Macron).[^cite_note-377] She is his former [La Providence High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyc%C3%A9e_la_Providence) teacher and is close to 25 years his senior.[^cite_note-378][^cite_note-Atlantic-379][^cite_note-380] They met during a theatre workshop that she was giving when she was a 39-year-old teacher and he was a 15-year-old student and classmate of her daughter.[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382][^cite_note-internaute-383] A romantic relationship started later, and his parents attempted to separate the couple by sending him away to Paris to finish the final year of his schooling, as they felt his youth made this relationship inappropriate.[^cite_note-Chrisafis-18][^cite_note-internaute-383] (The [age of consent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_consent_in_Europe) in France is 15.) The couple reunited after Macron graduated and were married in 2007.[^cite_note-internaute-383] She has three children from a previous marriage;[^cite_note-384] he has no children of his own.[^cite_note-385] Her role in Macron's 2017 presidential campaign has been considered pivotal, with close Macron allies stating that Brigitte Macron helped him to develop skills such as [public speaking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_speaking).[^cite_note-386]

His [best man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best_man) was [Henry Hermand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Hermand), a businessman who loaned €550,000 to Macron for the purchase of his first apartment in [Paris](22989.md) when he was Inspector of Finances. Hermand also let Macron use some of his offices on the [Avenue des Champs Élysées](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champs_%C3%89lys%C3%A9es) in Paris for his movement [En Marche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/En_Marche).[^cite_note-lemondeobit-387][^cite_note-libeobit-388]

In the [2002 French presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_French_presidential_election), Macron voted for [souverainist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Souverainism) [Jean-Pierre Chevènement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Pierre_Chev%C3%A8nement).[^cite_note-389] In 2007 Macron voted for [Ségolène Royal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A9gol%C3%A8ne_Royal) in the second round of the [presidential election](11105698.md).[^cite_note-390] During the [Socialist Party primary in 2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Socialist_Party_presidential_primary,_2011), Macron voiced his support for [François Hollande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Hollande).[^cite_note-391]

Macron plays the [piano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piano),[^cite_note-392] which he studied for ten years in his youth.[^cite_note-Neva_Editions_2015,_p.193-19] He especially enjoys the work of [Robert Schumann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Schumann) and [Franz Liszt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franz_Liszt).[^cite_note-393][^cite_note-394] Macron also skis,[^cite_note-395] plays [tennis](29773.md),[^cite_note-396] and enjoys [boxing](4243.md).[^cite_note-397] In addition to his native French, Macron speaks fluent English.[^cite_note-398][^cite_note-399]

In August 2017, a photojournalist was arrested and detained by the police for six hours after he entered the private residence where Macron was vacationing in [Marseille](40888948.md).[^cite_note-valeursactuellesporteplainte-400] Macron subsequently filed a complaint for "[harassment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harassment)".[^cite_note-valeursactuellesporteplainte-400] In September 2017, he dropped the complaint "as a gesture of appeasement".[^cite_note-lexpansionmacronretiresaplainte-401]

On 27 August 2017, Macron and his wife Brigitte adopted [Nemo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nemo_\(dog\)), a black Labrador Retriever-Griffon dog who lives with them in the Élysée Palace.[^cite_note-lefigunnouveauchienalelysee-402]

When he was a schoolboy, Macron decided to be baptised as a Catholic. In June 2018, prior to meeting [Pope Francis](1687680.md), he identified himself as an [agnostic Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lapsed_Catholic).[^cite_note-403][^cite_note-spotlight-404] In the same year he agreed to become an honorary canon of [St John Lateran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_John_Lateran), the cathedral of Rome.[^cite_note-spotlight-404]



Macron celebrating France's victory over [Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatia_national_football_team) in the [2018 World Cup final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_Final) in Moscow, Russia

A fan of [football](10568.md), Macron is a supporter of French club [Olympique de Marseille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympique_de_Marseille).[^cite_note-405] During the [2018 World Cup](13327177.md), he attended the semi-final between [France](250197.md) and [Belgium](679716.md) with the Belgian [King Philippe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippe_of_Belgium) and [Queen Mathilde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Mathilde_of_Belgium);[^cite_note-406] and at the [World Cup final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_Final) against [Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatia_national_football_team), he sat and celebrated alongside Croatian president [Kolinda Grabar-Kitarović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolinda_Grabar-Kitarovi%C4%87). Macron received widespread media attention for his celebrations and his interactions with the Croatian president.[^cite_note-407]

At a new year's celebration for military forces on 15 January 2026, Macron was seen with a bulging eye. The condition is known as a "[sub-conjunctival haemorrhage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sub-conjunctival_haemorrhage)". He chose to wear aviator sunglasses in public spaces to obscure this injury.[^cite_note-408] After gaining attention for wearing aviator sunglasses while speaking at the [World Economic Forum](172185.md) in [Davos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davos) on 20 January, demand for Henry Jullien sunglasses spiked. The brand's website crashed and its parent company, iVision, saw its [stock price](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stock_price) increase by 28%.[^cite_note-409][^cite_note-410]
